- **GS Stock Screener** — The week's most newsworthy desk: broke the team's 65+-run chronic WebSearch dateline wall for the first time, sourcing today's broad selloff to Intel's guidance reaction plus a hot CPI print from multiple same-day outlets — a real, durable process win. The real mark against it: its own "~$30B NVDA stake in Intel" figure inside that same report looks inflated against public record (~$5B) and went uncorrected across two of its own subsequent cycles after the trader flagged it — radical transparency on the big finding, not yet on the one specific number inside it.
- **Net for the week:** zero trades — the 16th consecutive week without one (last actual trade 9/3, now 58+ days), pool round-tripping from the week's open (~+0.29%) through the FOMC/GEHC/OMCL cluster clearing cleanly (no realized gains or losses, no structural break on either print) to today's Intel/CPI-driven selloff compressing the week's gains to a brief intraday low of +0.046% before a partial recovery to +0.32% by this distillation. Every contingency plan written in advance for this cluster (GEHC's, OMCL's, BR's earnings-window trigger) governed its print exactly as designed, with zero same-minute decisions — the clearest validation yet that pre-committing a reaction rule pays off precisely when the pressure to act in the moment is highest. No analyst call cost the book money for a 16th straight week. The week's other headline was operational, not analytical: the Balance-history file-size problem, raised for 9 consecutive weeks, was finally fixed this distillation (see Operational note) — a genuine process backlog closed, not just re-flagged. Heading into week 17: whether today's Intel/CPI-sourced selloff extends or reverses Monday; whether GS corrects its own unverified $30B figure; whether OMCL's thinned margin of safety ever actually trades through the $35.24 bear case; and whether BR's cadence recovers a second time.

**Analyst scorecard — Week 17 (2026-10-30 through 2026-11-06), condensed:** Zero trades, 17th consecutive no-trade week. A broad AI/semis selloff (no single-held-name structural cause) deepened through the week, compressing NVDA's buffer to the $180 contingency-plan-v2 line to a new tightest-ever reading (~1.52%, under 2% for the first time, 11/5) without the line ever actually being crossed — every check correctly applied "a decline alone, however tight the remaining buffer, is not the trigger," with a partial Friday bounce (11/6) widening the buffer back to ~3.9-4.0%. GS corrected neither its own unverified $30B NVDA/Intel figure flagged the prior week nor needed to — the item simply stopped recurring. No analyst call cost the book money for a 17th straight week. Heading into week 18: whether NVDA's buffer holds, deepens, or finally gets crossed, with the 11/17 print now inside the two-week earnings window for the first time.

**Analyst scorecard — Week 18 (2026-11-06 through 2026-11-13):**
- **JPM Earnings Analyzer** — The week's most consequential desk by volume of real, fast-moving fact: confirmed NVDA's 11/17 print date and falling implied move early in the week, then tracked the Huang resignation's downstream mechanics in real time — Kress as first-time CEO on the call, the House committee's 11/18 briefing request landing one day *after* the print, a materially above-guidance EPS consensus raising the bar for what "beat" even means. Clean, calendar-correct, appropriately risk-aware work throughout a genuinely unprecedented week for this book's largest position.
- **MS DCF Valuation** — Delivered the week's most consequential single model change: a full ground-up NVDA rebuild (not just a WACC bump) following the resignation, landing on a $119/sh base case (~27% overvalued) that corroborated rather than extended a WACC-only stress estimate from the day before — a useful cross-check that the quick stress-read had already captured most of the reasonable damage. Also rebuilt OMCL's DCF off confirmed Q3 actuals and held the $35.24 bear-case line firm under repeated GS pressure-testing rather than revising it reflexively — the line held, and so did the stock's price action relative to it (eventually).
- **BW Risk Assessment** — The week's sharpest single catch: independently found NVDA's board had opened an investigation into Huang on 11/8 — a materially bigger story than any desk's "voluntary congressional briefing" framing — and, separately, caught OMCL's first-ever sustained-close breach of the $35.24 line a day after it happened (10:31 ET, 11/13), the first time this book's cross-vetting has actually missed a trigger in real time rather than caught it live. Graded itself F for the miss, then earned an upgrade back to D as the team's three-re-read fix held — exactly the "name the failure, then earn the recovery" discipline rule 21 now credits as validated under real pressure.
- **GS Stock Screener** — Independently re-derived the Huang resignation from different source outlets the same morning MS first found it (second-source confirmation, not just repetition), and kept AVGO moving forward as the one not-yet-vetted top pick without over-claiming it was ready to size. Steady, confirmatory week.
- **BR Portfolio Builder** — Cut NVDA's pool target from 10% to 7% following the resignation (the right mechanism — policy revision, not a forced trade) and set a new 4-hour post-disqualifier reporting commitment, genuinely improving cadence through the crisis window (near-daily 11/11-11/13) after being named the book's most durable process weakness for 10+ consecutive weeks. Also closed the chronic cross-desk 10-year-Treasury input disagreement (~4.86% vs. MS's conflated ~5.10%) this week. The one unresolved thread: GEHC's +2.17pp allocation gap was reaffirmed "unchanged" for a 14th-plus consecutive report — correct each time, but worth a single explicit re-decision rather than a 15th reaffirmation (see Open questions).
- **Net for the week:** zero trades — the 18th consecutive week without one (last actual trade 9/3, now 71+ days) — through the single most consequential event in this book's history: a confirmed CEO resignation at its largest position, five days before that position's own earnings print. The pre-committed NVDA contingency plan v2 (including the governance disqualifier added just one day before it fired) held exactly as designed — no same-minute decision, a demanded cross-desk re-read, no reactive sell into the shock and no premature "it's cheaper" add (see rule 21, newly formalized this distillation). OMCL's own bear-case line saw its first real test too (a genuine sustained-close breach, not just a touch) and resolved the same way four-plus prior touches had, via repeated structural-break checks finding nothing. Pool ended the week at a modest accumulated loss (~-2.3% to -2.8% across Friday's reads), almost entirely NVDA-driven, no realized losses. No analyst call cost the book money for an 18th straight week — if anything, this was the valuation/risk team's best week yet, catching a story the broader financial press was still underselling. Heading into week 19: the 11/17 print itself (Kress's first call, T-1 as of this Monday's open), whether the governance overhang resolves or deepens, and whether BR's crisis-driven cadence improvement survives a calmer week the way it hasn't the two previous times it was tested.
